The lower bound stated in (5.3) is weaker than the statement of Theorem 1. The strategy to upgrade the inequality (5.3) into the quantitative estimate (2.6) relies on the observation that the inequality (5.3) is scale invariant in two dimensions (the constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_\delta $$\end{document}cδ does not depend on L). One can thus implement a hierarchical decomposition of the space and leverage on this property to improve the result. Specifically, we implement a Mandelbrot percolation argument which allows to cover (almost completely) the box \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda _L$$\end{document}ΛL by a collection \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Q}}$$\end{document}Q of disjoint boxes (see Fig. 2), all of which satisfy the property5.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{\tau _1, \tau _2 \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda }} \frac{1}{|\Lambda _L|} \sum _{v \in \Lambda } \left( \left\langle f_v \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda }^{\tau _1} - \left\langle f_v \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda }^{\tau _2} \right) \le \frac{C}{\root 4 \of {\ln \ln L}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supτ1,τ2∈SZd\Λ1|ΛL|∑v∈ΛfvσΛ1-fvσΛ2≤ClnlnL4.Once this is achieved, an application of the domain subadditivity property for the left-hand side of (5.4) (see Sect. 6.4) yields the bound5.5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {P}}\left( \sup _{\tau _1, \tau _2 \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda _L}} \frac{1}{|\Lambda _L|} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _L} \left( \left\langle f_v \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau _1} - \left\langle f_v \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau _2} \right) \ge \frac{C}{\root 4 \of {\ln \ln L}} \right) \le \exp \left( - c \sqrt{\ln L} \right) .\nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Psupτ1,τ2∈SZd\ΛL1|ΛL|∑v∈ΛLfvσΛL1-fvσΛL2≥ClnlnL4≤exp-clnL.The inequality (5.5) is slighlty weaker than Theorem 1, since the stochastic integrability (the right-hand side of (5.5)) can be improved. This is achieved by using a concentration argument implemented in Sect. 7.4.

We complete this outline with a remark regarding the rate of convergence obtained: it is related to the lower bound \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_\delta $$\end{document}cδ through the formula\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \inf \left\{ \delta \in (0, 1) \,: \, c_{\delta }:= \frac{1}{\ln L} \right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}infδ∈(0,1):cδ:=1lnL.The result of Lemma 7.5 gives the value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_{\delta }:= \exp \left( - \frac{C}{\delta ^4} \right) $$\end{document}cδ:=exp-Cδ4, which yields the rate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1/\root 4 \of {\ln \ln L}$$\end{document}1/lnlnL4.

Outline of the proofs of theorem 4 and Theorem 5

To simplify the presentation of the argument, we make the additional assumption that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h = 0$$\end{document}h=0. The proofs of Theorem 4 and Theorem 5 rely on a Mermin-Wagner type argument (see [53]) which allows to use the continuous symmetry of the model to upgrade the inequality (5.2): the upper bound we obtain is stated in Proposition 8.4 and (a simplified version of it) reads, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \{ 1, \ldots , n\}$$\end{document}i∈{1,…,n}5.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(\eta ) -{{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(-\eta ) ~ \Big \vert ~ \eta _{ \Lambda _{L/2}, i }\right] \le \frac{C}{L^2}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}EFELτ,0(η)-FELτ,0(-η)|ηΛL/2,i≤CL2,where the free energy is defined in (6.9) below, the expectation in the left-hand side of (5.6) is the conditional expectation with respect to the values of the i-th coordinate of the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η inside the box \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda _{L/2}$$\end{document}ΛL/2 (see Sect. 6.3).

The main features of the inequality (5.6) are the following:The right-hand side of (5.6) decays like \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C/L^2$$\end{document}C/L2, in contrast to the upper bound C/L of (5.2) in the case of general spin systems.The derivative with respect to the averaged field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\eta }_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}:= \left| \Lambda _{L/2}\right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} \eta _{v,i} $$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i:=ΛL/2-1∑v∈ΛL/2ηv,i of the left-hand side of (5.6) is explicit and satisfies the equality 5.7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}{} & {} \frac{\partial }{\partial {\hat{\eta }}_{L/2,i}} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(\eta ) - {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(- \eta ) ~ \Big \vert ~ \eta _{\Lambda _{L/2,i} }\right] \nonumber \\{} & {} \qquad = -\frac{1}{\left| \Lambda _L \right| } \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0}(\eta ) + \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0}(- \eta ) ~ \Big \vert ~ \eta _{\Lambda _{L/2},i }\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂∂η^L/2,iEFELτ,0(η)-FELτ,0(-η)|ηΛL/2,i=-1ΛL∑v∈ΛL/2Eσv,iΛLτ,0(η)+σv,iΛLτ,0(-η)|ηΛL/2,i. In particular, using the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta \rightarrow -\eta $$\end{document}η→-η invariance of the law of the random field, we see that the expectation of the right-hand side of (5.7) is equal to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-2{\mathbb {E}}\left[ \left| \Lambda _L \right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0} \right] $$\end{document}-2EΛL-1∑v∈ΛL/2σv,iΛLτ,0. The expectation of the spatially and thermally averaged magnetization can thus be characterized as the expectation of the derivative with respect to the variable \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}$$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i of the left-hand side of (5.6).In dimensions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d \le 3$$\end{document}d≤3, the fluctuations of the averaged field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}$$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i are of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^{-d/2}$$\end{document}L-d/2, and are thus larger than the right-hand side of (5.6). Combining this observation with a variational principle (see Sect. 8.1) yields the algebraic rate of convergence stated in Theorem 4. In dimension 4, the fluctuations of the averaged field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\eta }_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}$$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i are of the same order of magnitude as the right-hand side of (5.6), and we implement a Mandelbrot percolation argument similar to the one presented in Sect. 5.1 to obtain the result.

The right-hand side of (5.6) decays like \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C/L^2$$\end{document}C/L2, in contrast to the upper bound C/L of (5.2) in the case of general spin systems.

The right-hand side of (5.6) decays like \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C/L^2$$\end{document}C/L2, in contrast to the upper bound C/L of (5.2) in the case of general spin systems.

The derivative with respect to the averaged field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\eta }_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}:= \left| \Lambda _{L/2}\right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} \eta _{v,i} $$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i:=ΛL/2-1∑v∈ΛL/2ηv,i of the left-hand side of (5.6) is explicit and satisfies the equality 5.7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}{} & {} \frac{\partial }{\partial {\hat{\eta }}_{L/2,i}} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(\eta ) - {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(- \eta ) ~ \Big \vert ~ \eta _{\Lambda _{L/2,i} }\right] \nonumber \\{} & {} \qquad = -\frac{1}{\left| \Lambda _L \right| } \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0}(\eta ) + \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0}(- \eta ) ~ \Big \vert ~ \eta _{\Lambda _{L/2},i }\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂∂η^L/2,iEFELτ,0(η)-FELτ,0(-η)|ηΛL/2,i=-1ΛL∑v∈ΛL/2Eσv,iΛLτ,0(η)+σv,iΛLτ,0(-η)|ηΛL/2,i. In particular, using the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta \rightarrow -\eta $$\end{document}η→-η invariance of the law of the random field, we see that the expectation of the right-hand side of (5.7) is equal to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-2{\mathbb {E}}\left[ \left| \Lambda _L \right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0} \right] $$\end{document}-2EΛL-1∑v∈ΛL/2σv,iΛLτ,0. The expectation of the spatially and thermally averaged magnetization can thus be characterized as the expectation of the derivative with respect to the variable \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}$$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i of the left-hand side of (5.6).

The derivative with respect to the averaged field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\eta }_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}:= \left| \Lambda _{L/2}\right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} \eta _{v,i} $$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i:=ΛL/2-1∑v∈ΛL/2ηv,i of the left-hand side of (5.6) is explicit and satisfies the equality 5.7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}{} & {} \frac{\partial }{\partial {\hat{\eta }}_{L/2,i}} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(\eta ) - {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}^{\tau , 0}_{\Lambda _L}(- \eta ) ~ \Big \vert ~ \eta _{\Lambda _{L/2,i} }\right] \nonumber \\{} & {} \qquad = -\frac{1}{\left| \Lambda _L \right| } \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0}(\eta ) + \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0}(- \eta ) ~ \Big \vert ~ \eta _{\Lambda _{L/2},i }\right] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂∂η^L/2,iEFELτ,0(η)-FELτ,0(-η)|ηΛL/2,i=-1ΛL∑v∈ΛL/2Eσv,iΛLτ,0(η)+σv,iΛLτ,0(-η)|ηΛL/2,i. In particular, using the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta \rightarrow -\eta $$\end{document}η→-η invariance of the law of the random field, we see that the expectation of the right-hand side of (5.7) is equal to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-2{\mathbb {E}}\left[ \left| \Lambda _L \right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _{L/2}} \left\langle \sigma _{v,i} \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0} \right] $$\end{document}-2EΛL-1∑v∈ΛL/2σv,iΛLτ,0. The expectation of the spatially and thermally averaged magnetization can thus be characterized as the expectation of the derivative with respect to the variable \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda _{L/2},i}$$\end{document}η^ΛL/2,i of the left-hand side of (5.6).

We point out that there is a distinct difference between the two constructions. In the presence of a continuous symmetry, we do not rely on the criterion (5.4) to define which box should belong to the partition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Q}}$$\end{document}Q but rather on the inequality, for a box \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq \Lambda _L$$\end{document}Λ⊆ΛL,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| {\mathbb {E}}\left[ \frac{1}{\left| \Lambda \right| } \sum _{v \in \Lambda } \left\langle \sigma _v \right\rangle _{\Lambda _L}^{\tau , 0} ~ \Big \vert ~ {\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda } \right] \right| \le \frac{C}{\sqrt{\ln \ln L}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E1Λ∑v∈ΛσvΛLτ,0|η^Λ≤ClnlnL,where the conditional expectation is taken with respect to the averaged field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_\Lambda := \left| \Lambda \right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda } \eta _v$$\end{document}η^Λ:=Λ-1∑v∈Ληv. This difference accounts for the slightly better rate of convergence obtained in Theorem 5: we obtain the rate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1/\sqrt{\ln \ln L}$$\end{document}1/lnlnL instead of the rate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1/\root 4 \of {\ln \ln L}$$\end{document}1/lnlnL4 in Theorem 1.

Notations, Assumptions and Preliminaries

General notation

For each real number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a \in {\mathbb {R}}$$\end{document}a∈R, we use the notation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lfloor a \rfloor $$\end{document}⌊a⌋ to refer to the integer part of a. A box is a subset of the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v + \Lambda _L$$\end{document}v+ΛL for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v \in {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}v∈Zd and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L \in {\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}L∈N. We call the vertex v the center of the box \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda := v + \Lambda _L$$\end{document}Λ:=v+ΛL and the integer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(2L+1)$$\end{document}(2L+1) its side length. Given a box \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Λ⊆Zd of side length L and a real number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha > 0$$\end{document}α>0, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \Lambda $$\end{document}αΛ the box with the same center as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ and side length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lfloor \alpha L \rfloor $$\end{document}⌊αL⌋, and define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda _{\alpha L}:= \alpha \Lambda _L$$\end{document}ΛαL:=αΛL. Given a set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Λ⊆Zd and a vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v \in {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}v∈Zd, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{dist}\,}}(v, \Lambda ):= \min _{w \in \Lambda } |v - w|.$$\end{document}dist(v,Λ):=minw∈Λ|v-w|. For each set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Λ⊆Zd, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1_\Lambda $$\end{document}1Λ the indicator function of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ.

Given a function g defined on either \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {R}}$$\end{document}R, a subset \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Λ⊆Zd or the set of configurations and valued in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {R}}^m$$\end{document}Rm, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_1, \ldots , g_m$$\end{document}g1,…,gm its components. In particular, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_{v, 1}, \ldots , f_{v, m}$$\end{document}fv,1,…,fv,m the components of the observable \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_v$$\end{document}fv, and, in the case of continuous spin systems studied in Sect. 8, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma _{v, 1}, \ldots ,\sigma _{v, n}$$\end{document}σv,1,…,σv,n the components of the spin \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma _v$$\end{document}σv.

For each bounded set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Λ⊆Zd and each fixed boundary condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _0 \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda }$$\end{document}τ0∈SZd\Λ, we denote by6.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda }(\eta ):= \sup _{\tau _1, \tau _2 \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda }} \left| \frac{1}{|\Lambda |} \sum _{v \in \Lambda } \left( \left\langle f_v \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda }^{\tau _1} - \left\langle f_v \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda }^{\tau _2} \right) \right| , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}FlucΛ(η):=supτ1,τ2∈SZd\Λ1|Λ|∑v∈ΛfvσΛ1-fvσΛ2,and6.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda }^{\tau _0}(\eta ):= \sup _{\tau \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda }} \left| \frac{1}{|\Lambda |} \sum _{v \in \Lambda } \left( \left\langle f_v \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda }^{\tau } - Structure of the random field

Given an integer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \{1, \ldots , m \}$$\end{document}i∈{1,…,m} and a vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v \in {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}v∈Zd, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _i$$\end{document}ηi and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _{v, i}$$\end{document}ηv,i the i-th component of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _v$$\end{document}ηv respectively.

Given a bounded set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Λ⊆Zd, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _\Lambda = (\eta _{\Lambda , 1}, \ldots , \eta _{\Lambda , m})$$\end{document}ηΛ=(ηΛ,1,…,ηΛ,m) the restriction of the field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η to the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ. We use the decomposition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _\Lambda := ({\hat{\eta }}_\Lambda , \eta ^\perp _\Lambda )$$\end{document}ηΛ:=(η^Λ,ηΛ⊥) with\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\hat{\eta }}_\Lambda := \left| \Lambda \right| ^{-1} \sum _{v \in \Lambda } \eta _v \hspace{3mm} \text{ and }\hspace{3mm}\eta ^\perp _\Lambda := \eta _{\Lambda } - {\hat{\eta }}_\Lambda . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}η^Λ:=Λ-1∑v∈ΛηvandηΛ⊥:=ηΛ-η^Λ.More generally, given a map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w: \Lambda \rightarrow {\mathbb {R}}^m$$\end{document}w:Λ→Rm, we use the decomposition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _\Lambda := ({\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda }, \eta ^\perp _{w,\Lambda })$$\end{document}ηΛ:=(η^w,Λ,ηw,Λ⊥) with, for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \{ 1, \ldots , m\}$$\end{document}i∈{1,…,m},\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda , i}:= \frac{1}{\sum _{v \in \Lambda }w_i(v)^2} \sum _{v \in \Lambda } w_i(v) \eta _{v,i} \hspace{3mm}\text{ and } \hspace{3mm} \eta ^\perp _{w,\Lambda ,i}:= \eta _{\Lambda ,i}- {\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda , i} w_i. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}η^w,Λ,i:=1∑v∈Λwi(v)2∑v∈Λwi(v)ηv,iandηw,Λ,i⊥:=ηΛ,i-η^w,Λ,iwi.We set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda , i}=0$$\end{document}η^w,Λ,i=0 if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_i = 0$$\end{document}wi=0.

Since the field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η is assumed to be a standard m-dimensional Gaussian vector, all the random fields listed above are Gaussian. For each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \{ 1, \ldots , m \}$$\end{document}i∈{1,…,m}, the variances of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\eta }_{\Lambda , i}$$\end{document}η^Λ,i and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{w, \Lambda , i}$$\end{document}η^w,Λ,i are equal \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left| \Lambda \right| ^{-1}$$\end{document}Λ-1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1/\left( \sum _{v \in \Lambda } w_i(v)^2\right) $$\end{document}1/∑v∈Λwi(v)2 respectively. Moreover the fields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\eta }_\Lambda $$\end{document}η^Λ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta ^\perp _\Lambda $$\end{document}ηΛ⊥ are independent, and the fields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda }$$\end{document}η^w,Λ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta ^\perp _{w,\Lambda }$$\end{document}ηw,Λ⊥ are independent.

Given a function F depending on the realization of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η in the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ and an integer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \{ 1, \ldots , m \}$$\end{document}i∈{1,…,m}, we may abuse notation and write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F({\hat{\eta }}_\Lambda , \eta ^\perp _\Lambda )$$\end{document}F(η^Λ,ηΛ⊥), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F({\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda , i}, \left( \hat{\eta }_{\Lambda , j} \right) _{j \ne i}, \eta ^\perp _\Lambda )$$\end{document}F(η^Λ,i,η^Λ,jj≠i,ηΛ⊥) or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F({\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda }, \eta ^\perp _{w,\Lambda })$$\end{document}F(η^w,Λ,ηw,Λ⊥) instead of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F(\eta )$$\end{document}F(η) when we want to emphasize the dependence of the map F on a specific variable.

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \{1, \ldots , m \}$$\end{document}i∈{1,…,m}, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{\partial F}{\partial {\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda ,i}}(\eta )$$\end{document}∂F∂η^Λ,i(η) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{\partial F}{\partial {\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda ,i}}(\eta )$$\end{document}∂F∂η^w,Λ,i(η) the partial derivatives of the map F with respect to the variables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda ,i}$$\end{document}η^Λ,i and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{w, \Lambda ,i}$$\end{document}η^w,Λ,i respectively.

Notation for conditional expectation

Assume that the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η is defined on a probability space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\Omega , {\mathcal {F}}, {\mathcal {P}})$$\end{document}(Ω,F,P), and that we are given two random variables F and X depending on the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {E}}\left[ |F| \right] < \infty $$\end{document}E|F|<∞, and a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma $$\end{document}σ-algebra \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {F}}_1 \subseteq {\mathcal {F}}$$\end{document}F1⊆F. We denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {E}}\left[ F \, | \, X\right] $$\end{document}EF|X (resp. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {E}}\left[ F \, | \, {\mathcal {F}}_1\right] $$\end{document}EF|F1) the conditional expectation of F with respect to X (resp. with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {F}}_1$$\end{document}F1). Given an event A, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {P}}\left( A \, | \, X\right) := {\mathbb {E}}\left[ 1_{A} \, | \, X \right] $$\end{document}PA|X:=E1A|X (resp. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {P}}\left( A \, | \, {\mathcal {F}}_1\right) := {\mathbb {E}}\left[ 1_{A} \, | \, {\mathcal {F}}_1 \right] $$\end{document}PA|F1:=E1A|F1) the conditional probability. The random variable X will frequently be the fields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_\Lambda $$\end{document}η^Λ, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta ^\perp _\Lambda $$\end{document}ηΛ⊥, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{\eta }}_{w,\Lambda }, \eta ^\perp _{w,\Lambda }$$\end{document}η^w,Λ,ηw,Λ⊥, the restriction of the field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η to some set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ, or a combination of these options.

Since the random variable \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {E}}\left[ F \,|\, X\right] $$\end{document}EF|X depends only on the realization of X, we may write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {E}}\left[ F \, | \, X\right] (X)$$\end{document}EF|X(X) instead of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {E}}\left[ F \,| \,X\right] (\eta )$$\end{document}EF|X(η) when we wish to make the dependence on the random field explicit. Similarly, we may write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {P}}\left( A \,|\, X\right) (X)$$\end{document}PA|X(X) instead of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {P}}\left( A \,|\, X\right) (\eta )$$\end{document}PA|X(η).

Finally, in the conditional expectations, we may omit to display the dependency in the random field to simplify the notation (e.g., we will typically write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {\mathbb {E}}[ {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}_{\Lambda }^{\tau } ~\vert ~ {{\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda }}]$$\end{document}E[FEΛτ|η^Λ] instead of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ {\mathbb {E}}[ {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}_{\Lambda }^{\tau }(\eta ) ~ \vert ~ {{\hat{\eta }}_{\Lambda }} ]$$\end{document}E[FEΛτ(η)|η^Λ]).

The domain subadditivity property

In this section, we state a domain subadditivity property satisfied by the quantity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda }(\eta )$$\end{document}FlucΛ(η). The result is a direct consequence of the consistency property (2.5) and is used frequently in the proofs of Sects. 7 and 8.

Proposition 6.1

(Domain subadditivity property). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta > 0$$\end{document}β>0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta : {\mathbb {Z}}^d\rightarrow {\mathbb {R}}^m$$\end{document}η:Zd→Rm. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda _1', \ldots , \Lambda _N'$$\end{document}Λ1′,…,ΛN′ of be a collection of disjoint bounded subsets of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Zd and define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda ':= \cup _{j = 1}^N \Lambda _j'$$\end{document}Λ′:=∪j=1NΛj′. Then we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda '}(\eta ) \le \sum _{j = 1}^N \frac{\left| \Lambda _j'\right| }{\left| \Lambda ' \right| } {{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda _j'}(\eta ), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}FlucΛ′(η)≤∑j=1NΛj′Λ′FlucΛj′(η),as well as, for any integer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i \in \{ 1, \ldots , m\}$$\end{document}i∈{1,…,m},\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{\tau \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda '}} \sum _{v \in \Lambda '} \left\langle f_{v,i} \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda '}^{\tau } \le \sum _{j = 1}^N \sup _{\tau \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda _j'}} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _j'} \left\langle f_{v,i} \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda _j'}^{\tau } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supτ∈SZd\Λ′∑v∈Λ′fv,iσΛ′τ≤∑j=1Nsupτ∈SZd\Λj′∑v∈Λj′fv,iσΛj′τand\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \inf _{\tau \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda '}} \sum _{v \in \Lambda '} \left\langle f_{v,i} \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda '}^{\tau } \ge \sum _{j = 1}^N \inf _{\tau \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda _j'}} \sum _{v \in \Lambda _j'} \left\langle f_{v,i} \left( \sigma \right) \right\rangle _{\Lambda _j'}^{\tau }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}infτ∈SZd\Λ′∑v∈Λ′fv,iσΛ′τ≥∑j=1Ninfτ∈SZd\Λj′∑v∈Λj′fv,iσΛj′τ.

Free energy: definition and basic properties

The finite-volume free energy of the random system is defined below (and corresponds to the one introduced in (5.1)).

Definition 6.2

(Finite-volume free energy). Given a bounded domain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda \subseteq {\mathbb {Z}}^d$$\end{document}Λ⊆Zd, an inverse temperature \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta > 0$$\end{document}β>0, a boundary condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in {\mathcal {S}}^{{\mathbb {Z}}^d{\setminus } \Lambda }$$\end{document}τ∈SZd\Λ, a realization of the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η, we define the free energy by the formula\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}_\Lambda ^{\tau } \left( \eta \right) := -\frac{1}{\beta \left| \Lambda \right| } \ln Z_{\beta , \Lambda , \tau }^{\eta , \lambda } = -\frac{1}{\beta \left| \Lambda \right| } \ln \int _{{\mathcal {S}}^{\Lambda }} \exp \left( - \beta H^{\eta , \lambda }_{\Lambda }(\sigma ) \right) \prod _{v \in \Lambda } \kappa (d \sigma _v), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}FEΛτη:=-1βΛlnZβ,Λ,τη,λ=-1βΛln∫SΛexp-βHΛη,λ(σ)∏v∈Λκ(dσv),where the integral is computed over the set of configurations satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma = \tau $$\end{document}σ=τ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {Z}}^d\setminus \Lambda $$\end{document}Zd\Λ.

We next collect without proof some basic properties of the finite-volume free energy.

Proposition 6.3

(Properties of the free energy). For any bounded domain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} '$$\end{document}η,η′, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}_{\Lambda }^{\tau } \left( \eta \right) - {{\,\textrm{FE}\,}}_{\Lambda }^{\tau } \left( \eta ' \right) The proof of these results is a direct consequence of the formula for the free energy stated in Definition 6.2, the assumption \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|f_v(\sigma )| \le 1$$\end{document}|fv(σ)|≤1 on the noised observable, the fact that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_v$$\end{document}fv has range R and the inequality (2.2).

Convention for constants

Throughout this article, the symbols c and C denote positive constants which may vary from line to line, with C increasing larger than 1 and c decreasing smaller than 1. These constants may depend only on the strength of the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ, the parameter m, the constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_H$$\end{document}CH and the radius R. In the setup of spin system with continuous symmetry, they may depend on the strength of the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ, the dimension of the sphere n and the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi $$\end{document}Ψ.

Proofs for Two-Dimensional Disordered Spin Systems

In this section, we study the general spin systems presented in Sect. 2 and prove Theorem 1, Theorem 2, Corollary 2.2 and Theorem 3.

In Sect. 7.1, we introduce the notion of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-stability for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-Lipschitz convex function and quantify the Lebesgue and Gaussian measures of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-stability set (see Proposition 7.3 and Corollary 7.4 below).

The next four sections are devoted to the proof of Theorem 1 following the outline of Sect. 5: Sect. 7.2 contains the proof of the estimate (5.3), in Sect. 7.3, we implement the Mandelbrot percolation argument and prove the inequality (5.5), and in Sect. 7.4 we complete the proof of Theorem 1 (in the general case) by upgrading the stochastic integrability of (5.5). Finally Sect. 2.7 is devoted to the proof of the inequality (2.7) of Theorem 1 (in the translation-invariant setup).

Three remaining sections (Sects. 7.6, 7.7 and 7.8) are devoted to the proofs of Theorem 2, Corollary 2.2 and Theorem 3 respectively.

A notion of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-stability for real-valued convex functions

The following statement is a general result about real-valued convex functions; it asserts that if two \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-Lipschitz continuous, convex and differentiable functions are close (in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^{\infty }$$\end{document}L∞-norm), then their derivatives cannot be too distant from each other on a set of large Lebesgue measure. We first introduce the following set.

Definition 7.1

Fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda > 0$$\end{document}λ>0. For each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-Lipschitz, convex function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g: {\mathbb {R}}\rightarrow {\mathbb {R}}$$\end{document}g:R→R, and each parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r > 0$$\end{document}r>0, we define the set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} N_{\lambda , r}(g):= \left\{ g_1: {\mathbb {R}}\rightarrow {\mathbb {R}}\,: \, g_1~\text{ is } \text{ convex, }~\lambda \text{-Lipschitz } \text{ continuous, } \text{ differentiable } \right. \\ \left. \text{ and } \text{ satisfies }~ \sup _{t \in {\mathbb {R}}}\left| g(t) - g_1(t) \right| \le r \right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Nλ,r(g):=g1:R→R:g1isconvex,λ-Lipschitzcontinuous,differentiableandsatisfiessupt∈Rg(t)-g1(t)≤r.

We then define the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-stability set of the function g as follows.

Definition 7.2

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-stability set) For each triplet of parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \lambda , \delta , r > 0$$\end{document}λ,δ,r>0, and each function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g: {\mathbb {R}}\rightarrow {\mathbb {R}}$$\end{document}g:R→R convex, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-Lipschitz and differentiable, we define the set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{Stab}(\lambda , \delta , r, g):= \left\{ t \in {\mathbb {R}}\,: \, \exists g_1 \in N_{\lambda , r}(g) ~\text{ such } \text{ that }~ \left| g_1'(t) - g'(t) \right| > \delta \right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Stab(λ,δ,r,g):=t∈R:∃g1∈Nλ,r(g)suchthatg1′(t)-g′(t)>δ.

The next proposition estimates the Lebesgue measure of the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Stab}(\lambda , \delta , r, g)$$\end{document}Stab(λ,δ,r,g).

Proposition 7.3

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-stability for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-Lipschitz convex functions). There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C \ge 1$$\end{document}C≥1 such that, for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda > 0$$\end{document}λ>0, each function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g: {\mathbb {R}}\rightarrow {\mathbb {R}}$$\end{document}g:R→R convex, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-Lipschitz and differentiable, and each pair of parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r, \delta > 0$$\end{document}r,δ>0,7.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{Leb}\,}}\left( \textrm{Stab}(\lambda , \delta , r, g) \right) \le \frac{C \lambda r}{\delta ^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}LebStab(λ,δ,r,g)≤Cλrδ2.

Corollary 7.4

There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C \ge 1$$\end{document}C≥1 such that for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda $$\end{document}λ-Lipschitz, convex and differentiable function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} {R}}$$\end{document}g:R→R, each pair of parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ,r > 0$$\end{document}δ,r>0, and each variance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma ^2> 0$$\end{document}σ2>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\sqrt{2 \pi \sigma ^2}}\int _{\textrm{Stab}(\lambda , \delta , r, g)} e^{-\frac{t^2}{2\sigma ^2}} \, dt \le 1 - e^{- C \frac{\lambda ^2 r^2}{\sigma ^2 \delta ^4}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}12πσ2∫Stab(λ,δ,r,g)e-t22σ2dt≤1-e-Cλ2r2σ2δ4.

Proof

The proof is a consequence of the following inequality: there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C>0$$\end{document}C>0 such that, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \sigma ^2 \in (0,1)$$\end{document}σ2∈(0,1), any real number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \ge \sigma $$\end{document}α≥σ,7.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{A \subseteq {\mathbb {R}},\, {{\,\textrm{Leb}\,}}\left( A \right) \le \alpha } \frac{1}{\sqrt{2 \pi \sigma ^2}} \int _{A} e^{-\frac{t^2}{2 \sigma ^2}} \, dt = \frac{1}{\sqrt{2 \pi \sigma ^2}} \int _{-\frac{\alpha }{2}}^{\frac{\alpha }{2}} e^{-\frac{t^2}{2 \sigma ^2}} \, dt \le 1 - e^{- C\frac{ \alpha ^2}{\sigma ^2}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supA⊆R,LebA≤α12πσ2∫Ae-t22σ2dt=12πσ2∫-α2α2e-t22σ2dt≤1-e-Cα2σ2.Corollary 7.4 is then obtained by combining the inequality (7.8) with the value \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha = C \lambda r / \delta ^2$$\end{document}α=Cλr/δ2 and the estimate (7.1). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

A lower bound for the effect for the boundary condition on the averaged magnetization

In this section, we apply the result of Proposition 7.3 to the free energy associated with a discrete spin system and obtain the inequality (5.3). As it will be useful in the rest of the proof of Theorem 1, we establish the result both in the case of boxes (see (7.9)) and annuli (see (7.10)). The lower bound is stated in the following lemma, and we recall the notations \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda }(\eta )$$\end{document}FlucΛ(η), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda }^{ \tau _0}(\eta )$$\end{document}FlucΛ0(η), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda ,i}(\eta )$$\end{document}FlucΛ,i(η) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda ,i}^{\tau _0}(\eta )$$\end{document}FlucΛ,i0(η) introduced in Sect. 6.1, as well as the notation R for the range of the observables \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(f_v)$$\end{document}(fv).

Lemma 7.5

Fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d = 2$$\end{document}d=2, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta >0$$\end{document}β>0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda >0$$\end{document}λ>0, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L \ge 2$$\end{document}L≥2. \frac{C}{\delta ^4} \right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}PFlucΛL(η)<δ+CL>exp-Cδ4.Additionally, for each vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v \in \Lambda _L$$\end{document}v∈ΛL and each integer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L' \le \frac{L}{2}$$\end{document}L′≤L2 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v + \Lambda _{L'} \subseteq \Lambda _L$$\end{document}v+ΛL′⊆ΛL,7.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {P}}\left( {{\,\textrm{Fluc}\,}}_{\Lambda _L \setminus (v + \Lambda _{L'})}(\eta ) < \delta + \frac{C}{L} \right) > \exp \left( -
